```mermaid
    WRK -.->|StatsD UDP 9125| STATSD
    STATSD -.->|Scrapes HTTP 9102| PROM
    PROM -->|Datasource| GRAF
```

### Compute & Platform Layer (KinD Cluster)

Workloads are partitioned across Kubernetes namespaces (`airflow`, `keda`, `observability`, `ingress-nginx`, `cert-manager`):

| Component | Namespace | Replicas | Role |
|:---|:---|:---:|:---|
| **API Server** | `airflow` | 2 | Airflow 3 FastAPI/Uvicorn UI & REST API, serves web interface |
| **Scheduler** | `airflow` | 2 | Parses DAGs, schedules task instances, manages DAG run lifecycle |
| **DAG Processor** | `airflow` | 1 | Standalone process applying `dag_policy` dynamic RBAC policies |
| **Celery Workers** | `airflow` | 1–5 (auto) | Autoscaled dynamically by KEDA based on queued/running tasks |
| **Triggerer** | `airflow` | 1 | Monitors deferred tasks with async sensors and triggers |
| **Flower** | `airflow` | 1 | Real-time Celery monitoring dashboard |
| **StatsD Exporter** | `airflow` | 1 | Ingests native Airflow StatsD metrics and serves Prometheus endpoints |
| **git-sync** | `airflow` | sidecar | Pulls DAG files from GitHub every 30 seconds via SSH |
| **KEDA Operator** | `keda` | 1 | Event-driven autoscaler querying Airflow PostgreSQL task queue |
| **Prometheus** | `observability` | 1 | Time-series metrics engine scraping StatsD exporter every 5s |
| **Grafana** | `observability` | 1 | Real-time operational dashboard visualizing scheduler, tasks, and worker health |
| **NGINX Ingress** | `ingress-nginx` | 1 | Edge reverse proxy terminating TLS for all cluster services |
| **cert-manager** | `cert-manager` | 3 | Automated X.509 certificate provisioning via internal Root CA Issuer |

All components have **resource limits**, **PodDisruptionBudgets**, and
**NetworkPolicies** configured. The API Server is exposed via `NodePort 30080`,
mapped to `localhost:8080` through the KinD port mapping.

### External Infrastructure (Docker Containers)

These services run as standalone Docker containers on the host machine, connected
to the KinD `kind` network. Each is bridged into the cluster via a headless
Kubernetes Service with a static Endpoints resource pointing to `172.18.0.1`
(the KinD gateway IP).

| Service | Image | Host Port | In-Cluster DNS | Purpose |
|:---|:---|:---:|:---|:---|
| **PostgreSQL 16** | `postgres:16-alpine` | `5432` | `postgres.airflow.svc.cluster.local:5432` | Airflow metadata database |
| **Redis 7** | `redis:7-alpine` | `6379` | `redis.airflow.svc.cluster.local:6379` | Celery task broker |
| **Keycloak 26** | `quay.io/keycloak/keycloak:26.0.0` | `8081` | `keycloak.airflow.svc.cluster.local:8080` | OpenID Connect Identity Provider |
| **OpenLDAP** | `osixia/openldap:1.5.0` | `389` | `openldap.airflow.svc.cluster.local:389` | Enterprise directory (users & AD groups) |
| **phpLDAPadmin** | `osixia/phpldapadmin:0.9.0` | `8082` | — | Web GUI for LDAP management |
| **HashiCorp Vault** | `hashicorp/vault:1.15.6` | `8200` | `vault.airflow.svc.cluster.local:8200` | Secrets engine (KV v2, K8s Auth) |
| **MinIO** | `cleanstart/minio:latest` | `9000`/`9001` | `s3.airflow.svc.cluster.local:9000` | S3-compatible object storage for logs |

### Data & Request Flow

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant User as 👤 User (Browser)
    participant API as Airflow API Server
    participant KC as Keycloak (OIDC)
    participant LDAP as OpenLDAP
    participant SCH as Scheduler
    participant DP as DAG Processor
    participant Redis as Redis (Broker)
    participant Worker as Celery Worker
    participant PG as PostgreSQL
    participant Vault as HashiCorp Vault
    participant S3 as MinIO (S3)
    participant Gmail as Gmail SMTP

    User->>API: GET /login → "Sign in with Keycloak"
    API->>KC: OIDC Authorization Code Flow
    KC->>LDAP: Validate credentials (LDAP federation)
    LDAP-->>KC: Authenticated + group memberships
    KC-->>API: ID Token + Access Token (groups claim)
    API->>API: Map groups → Airflow roles (AUTH_ROLES_MAPPING)
    API-->>User: Authenticated session (filtered DAG list)

    Note over DP,PG: DAG Parsing (Background)
    DP->>DP: Parse DAG files from git-sync volume
    DP->>DP: Execute dag_policy() → bind owner to AD role
    DP->>PG: Store DAG metadata + access_control

    Note over User,Gmail: Task Execution
    User->>API: Trigger DAG Run
    API->>PG: Create DagRun record
    SCH->>PG: Pick up DagRun, create TaskInstances
    SCH->>Redis: Enqueue task to Celery
    Worker->>Redis: Dequeue task
    Worker->>Vault: Fetch secrets (K8s ServiceAccount auth)
    Worker->>Worker: Execute task
    Worker->>S3: Upload task logs
    Worker->>PG: Update TaskInstance state

    alt Task Fails
        Worker->>Gmail: Send failure alert (STARTTLS)
        Gmail-->>User: 📧 Email notification
    end
```

---

## TLS Ingress & Automated Certificate Management

In enterprise environments, internal web services are never exposed over raw HTTP or direct NodePorts. This lab implements a production **Ingress & TLS termination architecture** using:
- **NGINX Ingress Controller**: Edge reverse proxy routing HTTP/HTTPS traffic to internal services.
- **cert-manager**: Kubernetes-native certificate controller managing the full X.509 lifecycle.
- **Internal Enterprise Root CA**: A dedicated `ClusterIssuer` (`airflow-ca-issuer`) that automatically issues and rotates TLS certificates.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    subgraph Client["Developer Workstation"]
        REQ["HTTPS Request<br/><i>https://airflow.local</i>"]
    end

    subgraph Cluster["KinD Kubernetes Cluster"]
        ING["NGINX Ingress Controller<br/><i>Port 80/443</i>"]
        CM["cert-manager<br/><i>ClusterIssuer (airflow-ca-issuer)</i>"]
        SEC["Secret: airflow-tls-secret<br/><i>tls.crt + tls.key</i>"]

        SVC_AIRFLOW["Service: airflow-api-server:8080"]
        SVC_KC["Service: keycloak:8080"]
        SVC_GRAF["Service: grafana:3000"]
    end

    REQ -->|SNI: airflow.local| ING
    CM -->|Issues & Signs| SEC
    SEC -.->|Mounted into| ING
    ING -->|Reverse Proxy| SVC_AIRFLOW
    ING -->|Reverse Proxy| SVC_KC
    ING -->|Reverse Proxy| SVC_GRAF
```

### Ingress Routing & TLS Matrix

| Service | Short Domain (`/etc/hosts`) | Instant Wildcard URL (Zero Config) | Target Service | TLS Secret |
|:---|:---|:---|:---|:---|
| **Airflow Web UI** | `https://airflow.local` | `https://airflow.172.18.0.2.nip.io` | `airflow-api-server:8080` | `airflow-tls-secret` |
| **Keycloak SSO** | `https://auth.local` | `https://auth.172.18.0.2.nip.io` | `keycloak:8080` | `keycloak-tls-secret` |
| **Flower Monitor** | `https://flower.local` | `https://flower.172.18.0.2.nip.io` | `airflow-flower:5555` | `flower-tls-secret` |
| **Grafana UI** | `https://grafana.local` | `https://grafana.172.18.0.2.nip.io` | `grafana:3000` | `grafana-tls-secret` |
| **Prometheus UI** | `https://prometheus.local` | `https://prometheus.172.18.0.2.nip.io` | `prometheus:9090` | `prometheus-tls-secret` |
| **HashiCorp Vault** | `https://vault.local` | `https://vault.172.18.0.2.nip.io` | `vault:8200` | `vault-tls-secret` |
| **MinIO Console** | `https://minio.local` | `https://minio.172.18.0.2.nip.io` | `s3:9001` | `minio-tls-secret` |
| **phpLDAPadmin** | `https://ldap.local` | `https://ldap.172.18.0.2.nip.io` | `phpldapadmin:80` | `ldap-tls-secret` |

> [!TIP]
> **Two ways to access HTTPS:**
> 1. **Immediate Zero-Config**: Open the `.nip.io` URLs in your browser right now — no system edits required.
> 2. **Short Domains**: Run `sudo ./ctl hosts` to automatically append `172.18.0.2 airflow.local ...` to `/etc/hosts`.

## Worker Autoscaling — KEDA

Rather than maintaining a static pool of Celery worker pods, the cluster runs **KEDA (Kubernetes Event-driven Autoscaling)** to dynamically scale workers based on workload demand.

```mermaid
flowchart TD
    subgraph Airflow["Airflow Workload Activity"]
        TI["Task Instances Queued / Running"]
        PG[(PostgreSQL Metadata DB)]
        TI -->|State: queued / running| PG
    end

    subgraph KEDA_Control["KEDA Controller Loop"]
        SO["ScaledObject (airflow-worker)"]
        AUTH["TriggerAuthentication (DB secret)"]
        HPA["Horizontal Pod Autoscaler (HPA)"]

        AUTH --> SO
        SO -->|Polls every 5s| PG
        SO -->|Calculates replica count| HPA
    end

    subgraph Workers["Celery Worker Deployment"]
        W1["Worker Pod 1 (Always Warm)"]
        W2["Worker Pod 2 (Scaled on Demand)"]
        WN["Worker Pod N (Up to 5 max)"]

        HPA -->|Scale up / down| W1
        HPA -->|Scale up / down| W2
        HPA -->|Scale up / down| WN
    end
```

### Scaling Logic & Configuration

1. **Trigger Query**: KEDA polls PostgreSQL every 5 seconds executing:
   ```sql
   SELECT ceil(COUNT(*)::decimal / 16)
   FROM task_instance
   WHERE (state='running' OR state='queued')
     AND queue IN ('default')
   ```
2. **Capacity Ratio**: Celery workers run with concurrency `16`. When 16 tasks are active, 1 worker is required. When load bursts to 32 or 48 tasks, KEDA signals the HPA to instantly scale to 2 or 3 worker pods.
3. **Scaling Limits**:
   - `minReplicaCount: 1` — Keeps 1 warm worker pod running to eliminate cold-start latency for scheduled DAGs.
   - `maxReplicaCount: 5` — Safeguards cluster compute capacity while allowing 5x parallel throughput.
   - `cooldownPeriod: 30s` — Prevents rapid flapping after burst completion.

Inspect real-time autoscaler health with:
```bash
./ctl autoscaler
```

---

## Observability Stack — Prometheus & Grafana

Operational health is continuously monitored using an enterprise **Prometheus & Grafana observability pipeline**:

1. **StatsD Emission**: All Airflow containers (scheduler, worker, api-server, dag-processor, triggerer) emit fine-grained StatsD metrics over UDP to `airflow-statsd:9125`.
2. **Prometheus Metrics Bridge**: The `statsd-exporter` translates StatsD metrics into standard Prometheus metrics on port `9102`.
3. **Zero-Trust Network Policy**: A dedicated `allow-prometheus-scrape-statsd` NetworkPolicy permits pods in namespace `observability` to scrape metrics across namespace boundaries while preserving network isolation.
4. **Grafana Dashboards**: Grafana runs with Prometheus pre-configured as the default datasource and comes pre-loaded with an **Airflow Production Overview** dashboard.

### Key Metrics Monitored in Grafana

| Metric | Source | Purpose |
|:---|:---|:---|
| `airflow_scheduler_heartbeat` | Scheduler | Liveness & health of Airflow schedulers |
| `airflow_executor_open_slots` | Celery Executor | Available capacity in the Celery worker pool |
| `airflow_executor_queued_tasks` | Celery Broker | Backlog latency and pending task instances |
| `airflow_executor_running_tasks` | Celery Workers | Active parallel execution load |
| `airflow_ti_finish` (by state) | Task Instances | Success, failure, and retry rates |
| `airflow_dag_processing_total_parse_time` | DAG Processor | Detection of slow DAG parsing or parsing stalls |

Access the dashboard:
```bash
./ctl metrics
# Open https://grafana.local (or port-forward: kubectl port-forward svc/grafana 3000:3000 -n observability)
```

### Unified Alerting & Platform Notifications (Grafana + Prometheus)

Operational monitoring is paired with automated **Grafana Unified Alerting** routed to Gmail (`alerts@example.com`) via TLS/STARTTLS relay on `smtp.gmail.com:587`:

1. **Pre-Provisioned Contact Points**: `email-admin` contact point is loaded as code via `alerting.yaml` ConfigMap, referencing the cluster `grafana-smtp-secret`.
2. **Notification Policy**: Default root policy matches all alert groups (`AirflowAlertRules`), enforcing a 10s evaluation buffer, 30s group batching, and 1-hour alert deduplication/repeat intervals.
3. **Provisioned Alert Rules**:
   - **`AirflowSchedulerDown` (Severity: Critical)**: Continuously evaluates `sum(rate(airflow_scheduler_heartbeat[1m])) or vector(0) < 0.1`. If all schedulers cease heartbeat emission for > 1 minute, an alert email is dispatched.
   - **`AirflowDagImportErrors` (Severity: Warning)**: Evaluates `sum(airflow_dag_processing_import_errors) or vector(0) > 0`. Triggers when the DAG processor discovers syntax or import errors in the DAG folder.

#### Airflow DAG Failure Alerts vs. Grafana Platform Alerts

| Dimension | Airflow DAG Alerting (`./ctl test-alert`) | Grafana Platform Alerting (`./ctl test-grafana-alert`) |
|:---|:---|:---|
| **Primary Scope** | Pipeline & data task instance execution errors | Infrastructure & service health / cluster availability |
| **Alert Engine** | Airflow `SmtpNotifier` in DAG `default_args` | Grafana Unified Alerting + Prometheus queries |
| **Source Data** | Database task instance state (`FAILED`) | Time-series metrics from `statsd-exporter` (`9102`) |
| **Notification Channel**| Direct SMTP delivery via Celery worker pod | Alertmanager notification policy via Grafana pod |
| **Use Cases** | Downstream data missing, SQL syntax errors, API timeouts | Schedulers down, Celery queue backlog, broken DAG files |
| **Test Command** | `./ctl test-alert` | `./ctl test-grafana-alert` |

---

## RBAC Architecture — Owner-Based Dynamic Access Control

The RBAC system achieves **complete DAG isolation** between teams without
requiring DAG authors to hardcode any permissions. It uses a three-tier identity
chain.

### The Identity Chain: LDAP → Keycloak → Airflow

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    subgraph LDAP["OpenLDAP (Source of Truth)"]
        U1["uid=user_finance"]
        G1["cn=aid-finance-auth"]
        U1 -.->|memberOf| G1
    end

    subgraph KC["Keycloak (Identity Broker)"]
        FED["LDAP User Federation"]
        GM["Group Mapper"]
        FED -->|sync| GM
    end

    subgraph AF["Airflow (Policy Enforcement)"]
        RM["AUTH_ROLES_MAPPING<br/><i>webserver_config.py</i>"]
        DP["dag_policy()<br/><i>airflow_local_settings.py</i>"]
        DAG["finance_monthly_report<br/><i>owner: finance</i>"]
    end

    G1 -->|"OIDC groups claim"| GM
    GM -->|"groups: [aid-finance-auth]"| RM
    RM -->|"Airflow Role: aid-finance-auth"| AF
    DP -->|"access_control[aid-finance-auth]<br/>= {can_read, can_edit, can_delete}"| DAG
```

| Layer | Component | Responsibility |
|:---|:---|:---|
| **Directory** | OpenLDAP | Stores users and AD security groups (`aid-<keyword>-auth`) |
| **Identity Broker** | Keycloak | Federates LDAP users, issues OIDC tokens with `groups` claim |
| **Auth Gateway** | `webserver_config.py` | Maps OIDC `groups` claim to Airflow roles via `AUTH_ROLES_MAPPING` |
| **Policy Engine** | `airflow_local_settings.py` | `dag_policy()` extracts `owner` from DAG, builds `aid-<owner>-auth`, sets `access_control` |

### How It Works End-to-End

1. **LDAP defines the truth**: `user_finance` belongs to group `aid-finance-auth`
2. **Keycloak federates**: Syncs users/groups from LDAP, includes `groups` in OIDC tokens
3. **Airflow maps roles at login**: `AUTH_ROLES_MAPPING` converts `aid-finance-auth` → Airflow role `aid-finance-auth`
4. **DAG Processor applies policy**: `dag_policy()` reads `owner="finance"` from the DAG, constructs `aid-finance-auth`, grants `{can_read, can_edit, can_delete}`
5. **Result**: `user_finance` sees **only** `finance_monthly_report`. All other DAGs are invisible.

### DAG Authoring Convention

> [!IMPORTANT]
> DAG developers **never hardcode `access_control`** in DAG files.
> All permission binding is dynamic via the `owner` keyword.

The naming convention for AD groups is: **`aid-<keyword>-auth`**

```python
# dags/finance/finance_report.py
with DAG(
    dag_id="finance_monthly_report",
    default_args={"owner": "finance"},   # ← "finance" maps to "aid-finance-auth"
    tags=["finance", "reporting"],
    ...
):
```

| Owner keyword | AD Group (auto-derived) | Users with access |
|:---|:---|:---|
| `finance` | `aid-finance-auth` | `user_finance`, `alice` (admin) |
| `marketing` | `aid-marketing-auth` | `user_marketing`, `alice` (admin) |
| `logistics` | `aid-logistics-auth` | `user_logistics`, `alice` (admin) |
| `analytics` | `aid-analytics-auth` | `user_analytics`, `alice` (admin) |

---

## Secrets Management — HashiCorp Vault

Vault runs in **dev mode** as an external container, connected to the KinD cluster
via Kubernetes Auth method (zero-secret, ServiceAccount-based authentication).

```
secret/data/airflow/
├── variables/
│   ├── keycloak_admin_password
│   ├── keycloak_client_secret
│   ├── breakglass_password
│   └── alert_recipient
├── config/
│   ├── core/fernet_key
│   ├── webserver/secret_key
│   └── api/secret_key
└── connections/
    ├── postgres_default
    ├── smtp_default
    └── aws_default
```

Airflow accesses Vault via the `VaultBackend` secrets backend, configured in
`values.yaml` under `config.secrets`. Pods authenticate using their Kubernetes
ServiceAccount token — no static tokens or passwords are injected.

---

## Remote Logging — MinIO S3

Task execution logs are shipped to MinIO (S3-compatible object storage) via the
`apache-airflow-providers-amazon` provider. Configuration in `values.yaml`:

| Setting | Value |
|:---|:---|
| `remote_logging` | `True` |
| `remote_log_conn_id` | `aws_default` (points to MinIO) |
| `remote_base_log_folder` | `s3://airflow-logs` |
| MinIO endpoint | `http://s3.airflow.svc.cluster.local:9000` |

---

## Failure Alerting — Gmail SMTP

When a task fails, Airflow sends an email notification via Gmail SMTP (STARTTLS
on port 587). The alert includes the DAG name, task ID, attempt number, and a
link to the task log. Custom HTML/text templates are baked into the Docker image
at `/opt/airflow/config/email/`.

> [!NOTE]
> A 16-character Google App Password is required. The startup script prompts for
> it interactively or reads from the `GMAIL_APP_PASSWORD` environment variable.

## Quick Start

```bash
# 1. Clone the repository
git clone git@github.com:your-org/airflow-enterprise-lab.git
cd Learn_Airflow

# 2. One command to launch everything
./ctl start
# ↳ Creates KinD cluster
# ↳ Builds custom Airflow Docker image
# ↳ Starts OpenLDAP + phpLDAPadmin + Keycloak + PostgreSQL + Redis + Vault + MinIO
# ↳ Bridges all external services into the cluster via K8s Services/Endpoints
# ↳ Deploys Airflow via Helm chart
# ↳ Seeds AD group roles and base permissions into Airflow DB

# 3. Check everything is healthy
./ctl status

# 4. Open Airflow and log in with Keycloak SSO
#    → http://localhost:8080 → Click "Sign in with keycloak"

# 5. Tear down when done
./ctl stop
```

## CLI Reference (`./ctl`)

The `ctl` script is a portable, dependency-free bash CLI that manages the entire
lab lifecycle.

| Command | Description |
|:---|:---|
| `./ctl start` | Create KinD cluster, build image, start all infra, deploy Airflow, Ingress, KEDA, and Observability |
| `./ctl stop` | Delete KinD cluster and stop all external Docker containers |
| `./ctl status` | Show pod health across all namespaces, external container status, and endpoints |
| `./ctl build` | Rebuild the custom Airflow Docker image |
| `./ctl autoscaler` | Inspect KEDA worker autoscaler targets, HPA status, and database queue depth |
| `./ctl metrics` | Check Prometheus & Grafana health, dashboard URLs, and port-forward helpers |
| `./ctl ingress` | View Ingress routing, cert-manager TLS certificates, and local `/etc/hosts` DNS setup |
| `./ctl hosts` | Automatically add or check `/etc/hosts` DNS records for all secure services |
| `./ctl test` | Run CI/CD DAG governance and policy validation test suite |
| `./ctl test-alert` | Trigger the real failure drill DAG to verify Airflow SmtpNotifier Gmail delivery |
| `./ctl test-grafana-alert` | Trigger Grafana Alerting notification test to Gmail (`alerts@example.com`) |
| `./ctl test-auth` | Test Keycloak OIDC token issuance for all team users |
| `./ctl logs <target>` | Tail logs for `worker`, `scheduler`, or `keycloak` |
| `./ctl vault status` | Check Vault container health |
| `./ctl vault ui` | Print Vault Web UI URL and root token |
| `./ctl vault start` | Start Vault and seed initial secrets |
| `./ctl ldap status` | Show OpenLDAP and phpLDAPadmin container status |
| `./ctl ldap ui` | Print phpLDAPadmin URL and login DN |
| `./ctl ldap list-users` | List all users in the LDAP directory |
| `./ctl ldap list-groups` | List all AD groups and their members |
| `./ctl ldap add-user` | Create a new LDAP user and add to an AD group |
| `./ctl ldap backup` | Dump LDAP directory and archive snapshot to MinIO S3 (`s3://airflow-backups/ldap/`) |
| `./ctl ldap restore` | Restore LDAP directory from latest MinIO S3 backup and trigger Keycloak sync |
| `./ctl ldap sync` | Trigger Keycloak full LDAP sync |

A `Makefile` wrapper is also available for common commands (`make start`,
`make stop`, `make status`, `make build`, `make test-alert`, `make test-auth`).

## Project Structure

```text
.
├── ctl                                  # Portable CLI — manages full lab lifecycle
├── Makefile                             # Make wrapper for CI/CD-friendly commands
├── Dockerfile                           # Multi-stage Airflow 3 image (slim base + LDAP/auth/statsd deps)
├── requirements.txt                     # Python deps: celery, fab, postgres, redis, smtp, ldap, vault, s3, statsd
├── values.yaml                          # Helm chart values — executor, secrets, SMTP, git-sync, metrics, resources
│
├── constraints/
│   └── constraints-3.12.txt             # Pinned pip constraints for deterministic Python 3.12 builds
│
├── config/
│   ├── airflow_local_settings.py        # dag_policy() — dynamic owner-based RBAC cluster policy
│   ├── webserver_config.py              # Flask-AppBuilder OAuth — Keycloak OIDC provider + role mapping
│   └── email/
│       ├── content.html                 # Jinja2 HTML template for failure alert emails
│       └── subject.txt                  # Jinja2 subject line template
│
├── dags/
│   ├── analytics/
│   │   ├── analytics_etl.py             # Analytics ETL pipeline (owner: analytics)
│   │   └── failure_drill.py             # SmtpNotifier failure drill DAG (owner: analytics)
│   ├── finance/
│   │   └── finance_report.py            # Finance monthly report (owner: finance)
│   ├── logistics/
│   │   └── logistics_delivery.py        # Logistics fleet monitoring (owner: logistics)

├── tests/
```

---

## DAG Development Rules

> [!WARNING]
> These rules are enforced across the repository. Violating them will break
> the RBAC model or cause DAG parsing failures.

1. **No `@dag` or `@task` decorators** — Do not use the TaskFlow API. Always use
   explicit Operator classes (`PythonOperator`, `BashOperator`, etc.)
2. **Context manager syntax** — Define DAGs with `with DAG(...) as dag:`
3. **Explicit dependencies** — Chain tasks using bitshift operators (`task1 >> task2`)
4. **Owner keyword** — Set `default_args={"owner": "<keyword>"}` to match your
   team's AD group (`aid-<keyword>-auth`)
5. **No hardcoded `access_control`** — Permissions are applied dynamically by
   `dag_policy()` in the cluster policy
6. **Tags** — Use `tags=["<keyword>"]` for additional categorization (tags
   matching `aid-*-auth` or known keywords are also auto-mapped by the policy)

---

## Troubleshooting

<details>
<summary><strong>Pods stuck in CrashLoopBackOff</strong></summary>

Check logs for the failing pod:
```bash
kubectl logs -n airflow <pod-name> -c <container> --tail=100
```
Common causes:
- PostgreSQL or Redis not reachable (external container stopped)
- Vault secret sync failed (check `airflow-api-secret` exists)
- Docker image not loaded into KinD (`kind load docker-image ...`)
</details>

<details>
<summary><strong>Keycloak login redirects fail or loop</strong></summary>

Ensure Keycloak is healthy:
```bash
curl -s http://localhost:8081/realms/airflow/.well-known/openid-configuration | jq .issuer
```
The `KEYCLOAK_INTERNAL_URL` env var must resolve inside the cluster. Check the
`keycloak` headless Service and Endpoints:
```bash
kubectl get endpoints keycloak -n airflow
```
</details>

<details>
<summary><strong>User sees no DAGs after Keycloak login</strong></summary>

1. Verify the user's LDAP group membership: `./ctl ldap list-groups`
2. Verify Keycloak synced the groups: `./ctl test-auth`
3. Check that AD team roles exist in Airflow: `kubectl exec -n airflow deploy/airflow-scheduler -c scheduler -- airflow roles list`
4. Ensure the DAG has the correct `owner` in `default_args`
</details>

<details>
<summary><strong>Gmail alerts not sending</strong></summary>

1. Verify the SMTP secret exists:
   ```bash
   kubectl get secret airflow-smtp-secret -n airflow
   ```
2. Test with a real 16-char Google App Password (not the placeholder)
3. Check worker logs for SMTP errors: `./ctl logs worker`
</details>

<details>
<summary><strong>Vault connection errors in scheduler/worker logs</strong></summary>

1. Check Vault health: `./ctl vault status`
2. Verify Vault is on the KinD network: `docker network inspect kind`
3. Check the `vault` Service and Endpoints in Kubernetes:
   ```bash
   kubectl get endpoints vault -n airflow
   ```
</details>

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
copy_repo_to_txt.py
===================
Exports an entire codebase into a single formatted text file.
Includes:
  1. Repository metadata & summary statistics
  2. Architecture / Directory tree diagram
  3. Formatted contents of all text files (with clear separators & metadata)

Usage:
    python3 scripts/copy_repo_to_txt.py
    python3 scripts/copy_repo_to_txt.py -o repo_dump.txt
    python3 scripts/copy_repo_to_txt.py --root /path/to/repo --output output.txt
"""

import argparse
import fnmatch
import os
import sys
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path
from typing import List, Set, Tuple

# Default directory and file patterns to ignore
DEFAULT_IGNORED_DIRS: Set[str] = {
    ".git",
    "__pycache__",
    ".pytest_cache",
    ".mypy_cache",
    ".ruff_cache",
    ".venv",
    "venv",
    "env",
    "node_modules",
}

DEFAULT_IGNORED_FILES: Set[str] = {
    ".DS_Store",
    "Thumbs.db",
}

BINARY_EXTENSIONS: Set[str] = {
    ".png", ".jpg", ".jpeg", ".gif", ".ico", ".webp", ".bmp", ".tiff",
    ".pdf", ".zip", ".tar", ".gz", ".tgz", ".bz2", ".xz", ".7z", ".rar",
    ".pyc", ".pyo", ".pyd", ".so", ".dylib", ".dll", ".exe", ".bin",
    ".woff", ".woff2", ".ttf", ".eot", ".otf",
    ".mp3", ".mp4", ".wav", ".avi", ".mov", ".mkv",
}


def is_binary_file(file_path: Path) -> bool:
    """Heuristic check to determine if a file is binary."""
    if file_path.suffix.lower() in BINARY_EXTENSIONS:
        return True
    try:
        with open(file_path, "rb") as f:
            chunk = f.read(8192)
            if b"\0" in chunk:
                return True
    except (OSError, PermissionError):
        return True
    return False


def should_ignore(
    path: Path,
    root_path: Path,
    output_abs_path: Path,
    ignored_dirs: Set[str],
    ignored_files: Set[str],
    custom_patterns: List[str],
) -> bool:
    """Check if a file or directory should be excluded from processing."""
    try:
        abs_path = path.resolve()
        if abs_path == output_abs_path:
            return True
    except OSError:
        pass

    rel_path_str = str(path.relative_to(root_path)).replace("\\", "/")

    # Check exact directory name matches
    if path.is_dir() and path.name in ignored_dirs:
        return True

    # Check parent directory names
    for part in path.parts:
        if part in ignored_dirs:
            return True

    # Check exact file name matches
    if path.is_file() and path.name in ignored_files:
        return True

    # Check custom glob patterns against relative path or filename
    for pattern in custom_patterns:
        if fnmatch.fnmatch(rel_path_str, pattern) or fnmatch.fnmatch(path.name, pattern):
            return True

    return False


def build_tree(
    current_dir: Path,
    root_path: Path,
    output_abs_path: Path,
    ignored_dirs: Set[str],
    ignored_files: Set[str],
    custom_patterns: List[str],
    prefix: str = "",
) -> List[str]:
    """Recursively builds an ASCII architecture tree diagram."""
    lines: List[str] = []
    try:
        entries = sorted(
            [
                e for e in current_dir.iterdir()
                if not should_ignore(e, root_path, output_abs_path, ignored_dirs, ignored_files, custom_patterns)
            ],
            key=lambda e: (not e.is_dir(), e.name.lower())
        )
    except (PermissionError, OSError) as err:
        lines.append(f"{prefix}[Error reading directory: {err}]")
        return lines

    total = len(entries)
    for idx, entry in enumerate(entries):
        is_last = (idx == total - 1)
        connector = "└── " if is_last else "├── "
        display_name = f"{entry.name}/" if entry.is_dir() else entry.name
        lines.append(f"{prefix}{connector}{display_name}")

        if entry.is_dir():
            extension = "    " if is_last else "│   "
            sub_lines = build_tree(
                entry,
                root_path,
                output_abs_path,
                ignored_dirs,
                ignored_files,
                custom_patterns,
                prefix + extension,
            )
            lines.extend(sub_lines)

    return lines


def collect_files(
    root_path: Path,
    output_abs_path: Path,
    ignored_dirs: Set[str],
    ignored_files: Set[str],
    custom_patterns: List[str],
) -> List[Path]:
    """Collects all non-ignored files sorted by relative path."""
    collected: List[Path] = []
    for dirpath, dirnames, filenames in os.walk(root_path):
        current_dir = Path(dirpath)

        # Prune ignored directories in-place
        dirnames[:] = [
            d for d in dirnames
            if not should_ignore(current_dir / d, root_path, output_abs_path, ignored_dirs, ignored_files, custom_patterns)
        ]

        for fname in filenames:
            file_path = current_dir / fname
            if not should_ignore(file_path, root_path, output_abs_path, ignored_dirs, ignored_files, custom_patterns):
                collected.append(file_path)

    collected.sort(key=lambda p: str(p.relative_to(root_path)).lower())
    return collected


def format_size(size_bytes: int) -> str:
    """Format bytes into human-readable string."""
    for unit in ["B", "KB", "MB", "GB"]:
        if size_bytes < 1024.0 or unit == "GB":
            return f"{size_bytes:.1f} {unit}" if unit != "B" else f"{size_bytes} B"
        size_bytes /= 1024.0
    return f"{size_bytes:.1f} GB"


def export_repository(
    root_dir: str = ".",
    output_file: str = "repo_bundle.txt",
    extra_excludes: List[str] = None,
    max_file_size_kb: int = 1024,
) -> None:
    """Main function to export the repository into a single text file."""
    root_path = Path(root_dir).resolve()
    output_path = Path(output_file).resolve()

    if not root_path.exists() or not root_path.is_dir():
        print(f"Error: Root directory '{root_dir}' does not exist or is not a directory.", file=sys.stderr)
        sys.exit(1)

    ignored_dirs = set(DEFAULT_IGNORED_DIRS)
    ignored_files = set(DEFAULT_IGNORED_FILES)
    custom_patterns = extra_excludes or []

    print(f"🔍 Analyzing repository at: {root_path}")
    print(f"📁 Output target: {output_path}")

    # Collect files
    files = collect_files(root_path, output_path, ignored_dirs, ignored_files, custom_patterns)

    # Count directories
    dirs_count = 0
    for dirpath, dirnames, _ in os.walk(root_path):
        current_dir = Path(dirpath)
        dirnames[:] = [
            d for d in dirnames
            if not should_ignore(current_dir / d, root_path, output_path, ignored_dirs, ignored_files, custom_patterns)
        ]
        dirs_count += len(dirnames)

    total_bytes = sum(f.stat().st_size for f in files if f.exists())

    print(f"📊 Discovered {len(files)} files across {dirs_count} directories ({format_size(total_bytes)}).")
    print("🌲 Generating architecture tree...")

    # Build architecture tree
    tree_lines = [f"{root_path.name}/"]
    tree_lines.extend(build_tree(root_path, root_path, output_path, ignored_dirs, ignored_files, custom_patterns))
    tree_diagram = "\n".join(tree_lines)

    # Write to output file
    print("✍️  Writing bundle to file...")
    separator_major = "=" * 80
    separator_minor = "-" * 80

    with open(output_path, "w", encoding="utf-8") as out:
        # Header Metadata
        out.write(f"{separator_major}\n")
        out.write(f"REPOSITORY EXPORT BUNDLE\n")
        out.write(f"{separator_major}\n")
        out.write(f"Repository Name : {root_path.name}\n")
        out.write(f"Root Directory  : {root_path}\n")
        out.write(f"Generated On    : {datetime.now().strftime('%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S')}\n")
        out.write(f"Total Files     : {len(files)}\n")
        out.write(f"Total Folders   : {dirs_count}\n")
        out.write(f"Total Size      : {format_size(total_bytes)}\n")
        out.write(f"{separator_major}\n\n")

        # Section 1: Architecture Tree Diagram
        out.write(f"{separator_major}\n")
        out.write(f"SECTION 1: ARCHITECTURE TREE DIAGRAM\n")
        out.write(f"{separator_major}\n\n")
        out.write(f"{tree_diagram}\n\n")

        # Section 2: File Contents
        out.write(f"{separator_major}\n")
        out.write(f"SECTION 2: FILE CONTENTS\n")
        out.write(f"{separator_major}\n\n")

        for idx, file_path in enumerate(files, 1):
            rel_path = file_path.relative_to(root_path).as_posix()
            file_size = file_path.stat().st_size
            formatted_size = format_size(file_size)

            out.write(f"{separator_major}\n")
            out.write(f"[{idx}/{len(files)}] FILE: {rel_path}\n")
            out.write(f"SIZE: {formatted_size} ({file_size} bytes)\n")

            # Check max file size
            if file_size > max_file_size_kb * 1024:
                out.write(f"STATUS: Omitted (exceeds limit of {max_file_size_kb} KB)\n")
                out.write(f"{separator_major}\n\n")
                continue

            # Check binary
            if is_binary_file(file_path):
                out.write(f"STATUS: Binary file (raw content omitted)\n")
                out.write(f"{separator_major}\n\n")
                continue

            # Text content
            out.write(f"{separator_major}\n")
            try:
                with open(file_path, "r", encoding="utf-8", errors="replace") as f:
                    content = f.read()
                    out.write(content)
                    if not content.endswith("\n"):
                        out.write("\n")
            except Exception as e:
                out.write(f"[Error reading file: {e}]\n")
            out.write("\n")

    output_size = output_path.stat().st_size
    print(f"✅ Repository successfully exported!")
    print(f"📄 Output file: {output_path} ({format_size(output_size)})")


def parse_args():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        description="Copy entire repository into a single text file with architecture tree diagram and file contents."
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "-r", "--root",
        default=".",
        help="Path to repository root directory (default: current directory)."
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "-o", "--output",
        default="repo_bundle.txt",
        help="Path for destination text file (default: repo_bundle.txt)."
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "-e", "--exclude",
        action="append",
        default=[],
        help="Additional glob pattern or directory to exclude (can be specified multiple times)."
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--max-size-kb",
        type=int,
        default=1024,
        help="Maximum size in KB for individual files before skipping content (default: 1024 KB / 1 MB)."
    )
    return parser.parse_args()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    args = parse_args()
    export_repository(
        root_dir=args.root,
        output_file=args.output,
        extra_excludes=args.exclude,
        max_file_size_kb=args.max_size_kb,
    )

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# ==============================================================================
# Setup & Bridge External PostgreSQL & Redis for Airflow
# Runs PostgreSQL 16 and Redis 7 as external services outside the KinD cluster,
# matching enterprise architecture where stateful services live outside compute.
# ==============================================================================
set -euo pipefail

ROOT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
POSTGRES_CONTAINER="airflow-postgres"
REDIS_CONTAINER="airflow-redis"
NAMESPACE="airflow"

# Colors
CYAN='\033[0;36m'
GREEN='\033[0;32m'
YELLOW='\033[1;33m'
RED='\033[0;31m'
NC='\033[0m'

echo -e "${CYAN}=== 1. Starting External PostgreSQL 16 Service ===${NC}"
if ! docker ps --filter "name=^/${POSTGRES_CONTAINER}$" --format '{{.Names}}' | grep -qx "${POSTGRES_CONTAINER}"; then
    if docker ps -a --filter "name=^/${POSTGRES_CONTAINER}$" --format '{{.Names}}' | grep -qx "${POSTGRES_CONTAINER}"; then
        echo "Starting existing '${POSTGRES_CONTAINER}' container..."
        docker start "${POSTGRES_CONTAINER}"
    else
        echo "Launching external PostgreSQL 16 container '${POSTGRES_CONTAINER}'..."
        docker run -d \
            --name "${POSTGRES_CONTAINER}" \
            --restart unless-stopped \
            -p 5432:5432 \
            -e POSTGRES_USER=airflow \
            -e POSTGRES_PASSWORD=airflow \
            -e POSTGRES_DB=airflow \
            -v airflow-postgres-data:/var/lib/postgresql/data \
            postgres:16-alpine
    fi
else
    echo "External PostgreSQL container '${POSTGRES_CONTAINER}' is already running."
fi

echo -e "${CYAN}=== 2. Starting External Redis 7 Broker Service ===${NC}"
if ! docker ps --filter "name=^/${REDIS_CONTAINER}$" --format '{{.Names}}' | grep -qx "${REDIS_CONTAINER}"; then
    if docker ps -a --filter "name=^/${REDIS_CONTAINER}$" --format '{{.Names}}' | grep -qx "${REDIS_CONTAINER}"; then
        echo "Starting existing '${REDIS_CONTAINER}' container..."
        docker start "${REDIS_CONTAINER}"
    else
        echo "Launching external Redis 7 container '${REDIS_CONTAINER}'..."
        docker run -d \
            --name "${REDIS_CONTAINER}" \
            --restart unless-stopped \
            -p 6379:6379 \
            -v airflow-redis-data:/data \
            redis:7-alpine
    fi
else
    echo "External Redis container '${REDIS_CONTAINER}' is already running."
fi

echo "Waiting for PostgreSQL to be ready..."
for i in {1..30}; do
    if docker exec "${POSTGRES_CONTAINER}" pg_isready -U airflow -d airflow >/dev/null 2>&1; then
        echo -e "${GREEN}PostgreSQL is healthy and accepting connections.${NC}"
        break
    fi
    sleep 1
done

echo "Waiting for Redis to be ready..."
for i in {1..30}; do
    if docker exec "${REDIS_CONTAINER}" redis-cli ping | grep -q "PONG"; then
        echo -e "${GREEN}Redis is healthy and accepting connections.${NC}"
        break
    fi
    sleep 1
done

echo -e "${CYAN}=== 3. Mapping External DB & Broker into Kubernetes ===${NC}"
if kubectl cluster-info >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    kubectl create namespace "${NAMESPACE}" --dry-run=client -o yaml | kubectl apply -f -
    kubectl apply -f "${ROOT_DIR}/k8s/postgres/service.yaml"
    kubectl apply -f "${ROOT_DIR}/k8s/redis/service.yaml"
    echo -e "${GREEN}Services 'postgres' and 'redis' mapped in namespace '${NAMESPACE}'.${NC}"

    # Create connection secrets for Airflow Helm chart
    kubectl create secret generic airflow-metadata-secret \
        --from-literal=connection="postgresql+psycopg2://airflow:airflow@postgres.airflow.svc.cluster.local:5432/airflow" \
        -n "${NAMESPACE}" --dry-run=client -o yaml | kubectl apply -f -

    kubectl create secret generic airflow-broker-secret \
        --from-literal=connection="redis://redis.airflow.svc.cluster.local:6379/0" \
        -n "${NAMESPACE}" --dry-run=client -o yaml | kubectl apply -f -
    echo -e "${GREEN}Kubernetes secrets 'airflow-metadata-secret' and 'airflow-broker-secret' created.${NC}"
else
    echo -e "${YELLOW}Kubernetes cluster not detected yet; skipping k8s service mapping.${NC}"
fi

echo -e "${GREEN}External Database and Broker services are ready!${NC}"

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# ==============================================================================
# Helper to configure /etc/hosts for Airflow Lab TLS Ingress
# Maps internal .local hostnames to the KinD control-plane IP.
# ==============================================================================
set -euo pipefail

CLUSTER_NAME="airflow-local"
NODE_IP=$(docker inspect "${CLUSTER_NAME}-control-plane" --format '{{range .NetworkSettings.Networks}}{{.IPAddress}}{{end}}' 2>/dev/null || echo "172.18.0.2")

HOSTS_LINE="${NODE_IP} airflow.local auth.local flower.local grafana.local prometheus.local vault.local minio.local ldap.local"

if grep -q "airflow.local" /etc/hosts; then
    echo "Entry for 'airflow.local' already exists in /etc/hosts:"
    grep "airflow.local" /etc/hosts
    exit 0
fi

if [[ $EUID -ne 0 ]]; then
    echo "Root privileges required to update /etc/hosts."
    echo "Run with sudo:"
    echo "  sudo ./ctl hosts"
    echo ""
    echo "Or manually execute:"
    echo "  echo \"${HOSTS_LINE}\" | sudo tee -a /etc/hosts"
    exit 1
fi

echo "${HOSTS_LINE}" >> /etc/hosts
echo "Successfully added DNS mappings to /etc/hosts:"
echo "  ${HOSTS_LINE}"

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# ==============================================================================
# Setup NGINX Ingress Controller & cert-manager TLS for Airflow
# Terminates TLS with automated CA-issued certificates for:
# - airflow.local (Airflow Web UI & API Server)
# - auth.local    (Keycloak SSO Identity Provider)
# - flower.local  (Celery Monitoring)
# - grafana.local (Observability Dashboards)
# ==============================================================================
set -euo pipefail

ROOT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
CLUSTER_NAME="airflow-local"

# Colors
CYAN='\033[0;36m'
GREEN='\033[0;32m'
YELLOW='\033[1;33m'
NC='\033[0m'

echo -e "${CYAN}=== 1. Ensuring KinD Node Ingress Labels ===${NC}"
kubectl label node "${CLUSTER_NAME}-control-plane" ingress-ready=true --overwrite 2>/dev/null || true

echo -e "${CYAN}=== 2. Deploying cert-manager for TLS Management ===${NC}"
if ! helm list -n cert-manager 2>/dev/null | grep -qx "cert-manager"; then
    echo "Adding Jetstack Helm repository..."
    helm repo add jetstack https://charts.jetstack.io 2>/dev/null || true
    helm repo update jetstack

    echo "Installing cert-manager with CRDs..."
    helm upgrade --install cert-manager jetstack/cert-manager \
        --namespace cert-manager \
        --create-namespace \
        --set crds.enabled=true \
        --wait --timeout=120s
else
    echo "cert-manager is already installed."
fi

echo -e "${CYAN}=== 3. Deploying NGINX Ingress Controller ===${NC}"
if ! kubectl get pods -n ingress-nginx -l app.kubernetes.io/component=controller >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    echo "Applying NGINX Ingress Controller for KinD..."
    kubectl apply -f https://raw.githubusercontent.com/kubernetes/ingress-nginx/main/deploy/static/provider/kind/deploy.yaml
fi

echo "Waiting for NGINX Ingress Controller rollout..."
kubectl wait --namespace ingress-nginx \
    --for=condition=ready pod \
    --selector=app.kubernetes.io/component=controller \
    --timeout=120s 2>/dev/null || true

echo -e "${CYAN}=== 4. Applying ClusterIssuer & Ingress Resources ===${NC}"
kubectl apply -f "${ROOT_DIR}/k8s/ingress/cluster-issuer.yaml"
kubectl apply -f "${ROOT_DIR}/k8s/ingress/airflow-ingress.yaml"

# Retrieve node IP for direct access
NODE_IP=$(docker inspect "${CLUSTER_NAME}-control-plane" --format '{{range .NetworkSettings.Networks}}{{.IPAddress}}{{end}}' 2>/dev/null || echo "127.0.0.1")

echo ""
echo -e "${GREEN}======================================================================${NC}"
echo -e "${GREEN}TLS Ingress is fully configured with automated cert-manager CA!${NC}"
echo -e "${GREEN}======================================================================${NC}"
echo "Add the following entry to your /etc/hosts for seamless browser access:"
echo -e "  ${YELLOW}${NODE_IP} airflow.local auth.local flower.local grafana.local prometheus.local${NC}"
echo ""
echo "Secure Endpoints (HTTPS):"
echo -e "  Airflow Web UI:  ${CYAN}https://airflow.local${NC}"
echo -e "  Keycloak SSO:    ${CYAN}https://auth.local${NC}"
echo -e "  Flower UI:       ${CYAN}https://flower.local${NC}"
echo -e "  Grafana UI:      ${CYAN}https://grafana.local${NC}"
echo -e "  Prometheus UI:   ${CYAN}https://prometheus.local${NC}"
echo "======================================================================"

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# ==============================================================================
# Setup KEDA (Kubernetes Event-driven Autoscaling) for Airflow Celery Workers
# Provisions KEDA operator and attaches ScaledObject to scale workers on demand.
# ==============================================================================
set -euo pipefail

ROOT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
NAMESPACE="airflow"
KEDA_NAMESPACE="keda"

# Colors
CYAN='\033[0;36m'
GREEN='\033[0;32m'
YELLOW='\033[1;33m'
NC='\033[0m'

echo -e "${CYAN}=== Setting up KEDA Worker Autoscaler ===${NC}"

# 1. Install KEDA operator if not already deployed
if ! helm list -n "${KEDA_NAMESPACE}" 2>/dev/null | grep -qx "keda"; then
    echo "Adding KEDA Helm repository..."
    helm repo add kedacore https://kedacore.github.io/charts 2>/dev/null || true
    helm repo update kedacore

    echo "Deploying KEDA operator to namespace '${KEDA_NAMESPACE}'..."
    helm upgrade --install keda kedacore/keda \
        --namespace "${KEDA_NAMESPACE}" \
        --create-namespace \
        --wait --timeout=120s
else
    echo "KEDA operator is already installed in namespace '${KEDA_NAMESPACE}'."
fi

# 2. Apply ScaledObject and TriggerAuthentication in Airflow namespace
echo "Applying Airflow worker ScaledObject and TriggerAuthentication..."
kubectl apply -f "${ROOT_DIR}/k8s/keda/keda-scaledobject.yaml"

echo -e "${GREEN}KEDA Worker Autoscaler successfully configured!${NC}"

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# ==============================================================================
# Setup & Bridge External Keycloak Identity Provider for Airflow
# Runs Keycloak as an external Docker container outside KinD cluster,
# matching enterprise architecture where IdP/IAM is a central external service.
# ==============================================================================
set -euo pipefail

ROOT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
KEYCLOAK_CONTAINER="airflow-keycloak"
KEYCLOAK_PORT="8081"
NAMESPACE="airflow"

# Colors
CYAN='\033[0;36m'
GREEN='\033[0;32m'
YELLOW='\033[1;33m'
RED='\033[0;31m'
NC='\033[0m'

echo -e "${CYAN}=== 1. Starting External Keycloak Identity Provider Container ===${NC}"
if ! docker ps --filter "name=^/${KEYCLOAK_CONTAINER}$" --format '{{.Names}}' | grep -qx "${KEYCLOAK_CONTAINER}"; then
    if docker ps -a --filter "name=^/${KEYCLOAK_CONTAINER}$" --format '{{.Names}}' | grep -qx "${KEYCLOAK_CONTAINER}"; then
        echo "Starting existing '${KEYCLOAK_CONTAINER}' container..."
        docker start "${KEYCLOAK_CONTAINER}"
    else
        echo "Launching external Keycloak container '${KEYCLOAK_CONTAINER}' on port ${KEYCLOAK_PORT}..."
        docker run -d \
            --name "${KEYCLOAK_CONTAINER}" \
            --restart unless-stopped \
            -p "${KEYCLOAK_PORT}:8080" \
            -e KEYCLOAK_ADMIN=admin \
            -e KEYCLOAK_ADMIN_PASSWORD=admin \
            -e KC_HTTP_PORT=8080 \
            -e KC_HOSTNAME="http://localhost:${KEYCLOAK_PORT}" \
            -e KC_HOSTNAME_STRICT="false" \
            -e KC_HOSTNAME_BACKCHANNEL_DYNAMIC="true" \
            -e KC_HTTP_ENABLED="true" \
            -e KC_HEALTH_ENABLED="true" \
            -v "${ROOT_DIR}/k8s/keycloak/realm-airflow.json:/opt/keycloak/data/import/airflow-realm.json:ro" \
            quay.io/keycloak/keycloak:26.0.0 \
            start-dev --import-realm
    fi
else
    echo "External Keycloak container '${KEYCLOAK_CONTAINER}' is already running."
fi

# Connect Keycloak container to KinD network if present
if docker network inspect kind >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    docker network connect kind "${KEYCLOAK_CONTAINER}" 2>/dev/null || true
fi

echo "Waiting for Keycloak to be ready at http://localhost:${KEYCLOAK_PORT}..."
for i in {1..60}; do
    if curl -s -f "http://localhost:${KEYCLOAK_PORT}/realms/airflow/.well-known/openid-configuration" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
        echo -e "${GREEN}Keycloak is healthy and realm 'airflow' is ready.${NC}"
        break
    fi
    sleep 2
done

echo "Ensuring Airflow client in Keycloak permits all Ingress redirect URIs..."
python3 -c '
import urllib.request, urllib.parse, json

data = urllib.parse.urlencode({
    "client_id": "admin-cli",
    "username": "admin",
    "password": "admin",
    "grant_type": "password"
}).encode()

try:
    req = urllib.request.Request("http://localhost:'"${KEYCLOAK_PORT}"'/realms/master/protocol/openid-connect/token", data=data)
    res = urllib.request.urlopen(req)
    token = json.loads(res.read())["access_token"]

    req2 = urllib.request.Request("http://localhost:'"${KEYCLOAK_PORT}"'/admin/realms/airflow/clients?clientId=airflow", headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {token}"})
    res2 = urllib.request.urlopen(req2)
    clients = json.loads(res2.read())
    if clients:
        client = clients[0]
        client["redirectUris"] = [
            "http://localhost:8080/*",
            "https://localhost:8080/*",
            "http://localhost:8080/oauth-authorized/keycloak",
            "http://airflow.local/*",
            "https://airflow.local/*",
            "http://airflow.local/oauth-authorized/keycloak",
            "https://airflow.local/oauth-authorized/keycloak",
            "http://airflow.172.18.0.2.nip.io/*",
            "https://airflow.172.18.0.2.nip.io/*",
            "*"
        ]
        client["webOrigins"] = ["*"]
        put_req = urllib.request.Request(
            f"http://localhost:'"${KEYCLOAK_PORT}"'/admin/realms/airflow/clients/{client[\"id\"]}",
            data=json.dumps(client).encode(),
            headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {token}", "Content-Type": "application/json"},
            method="PUT"
        )
        urllib.request.urlopen(put_req)
except Exception:
    pass
' 2>/dev/null || true

echo -e "${CYAN}=== 2. Mapping External Keycloak into Kubernetes ===${NC}"
if kubectl cluster-info >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    kubectl create namespace "${NAMESPACE}" --dry-run=client -o yaml | kubectl apply -f -
    kubectl apply -f "${ROOT_DIR}/k8s/keycloak/service.yaml"
    echo -e "${GREEN}Service 'keycloak' mapped in namespace '${NAMESPACE}' to external host container.${NC}"
else
    echo -e "${YELLOW}Kubernetes cluster not detected yet; skipping k8s service mapping.${NC}"
fi

echo -e "${GREEN}External Keycloak Identity Provider is ready!${NC}"

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# ==============================================================================
# Setup & Bridge External OpenLDAP & phpLDAPadmin for Airflow
# Runs OpenLDAP as an external Docker container outside KinD cluster,
# simulating enterprise Active Directory / LDAP user and group management.
# ==============================================================================
set -euo pipefail

ROOT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
LDAP_CONTAINER="airflow-openldap"
PHPLDAPADMIN_CONTAINER="airflow-phpldapadmin"
LDAP_PORT="389"
PHPLDAPADMIN_PORT="8082"
NAMESPACE="airflow"

# Colors
CYAN='\033[0;36m'
GREEN='\033[0;32m'
YELLOW='\033[1;33m'
RED='\033[0;31m'
NC='\033[0m'

echo -e "${CYAN}=== 1. Starting External OpenLDAP Directory Container ===${NC}"
if ! docker ps --filter "name=^/${LDAP_CONTAINER}$" --format '{{.Names}}' | grep -qx "${LDAP_CONTAINER}"; then
    if docker ps -a --filter "name=^/${LDAP_CONTAINER}$" --format '{{.Names}}' | grep -qx "${LDAP_CONTAINER}"; then
        echo "Starting existing '${LDAP_CONTAINER}' container..."
        docker start "${LDAP_CONTAINER}"
    else
        echo "Launching external OpenLDAP container '${LDAP_CONTAINER}' on port ${LDAP_PORT}..."
        docker run -d \
            --name "${LDAP_CONTAINER}" \
            --restart unless-stopped \
            -p "${LDAP_PORT}:389" \
            -v airflow-ldap-data:/var/lib/ldap \
            -v airflow-ldap-config:/etc/ldap/slapd.d \
            -e LDAP_ORGANISATION="Example Inc." \
            -e LDAP_DOMAIN="example.org" \
            -e LDAP_ADMIN_PASSWORD="admin" \
            osixia/openldap:1.5.0
    fi
else
    echo "External OpenLDAP container '${LDAP_CONTAINER}' is already running."
fi

# Connect OpenLDAP container to KinD network if present
if docker network inspect kind >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    docker network connect kind "${LDAP_CONTAINER}" 2>/dev/null || true
fi

echo "Waiting for OpenLDAP to be ready on port ${LDAP_PORT}..."
for i in {1..30}; do
    if docker exec "${LDAP_CONTAINER}" ldapsearch -x -H ldap://localhost -b "dc=example,dc=org" -D "cn=admin,dc=example,dc=org" -w admin >/dev/null 2>&1; then
        echo -e "${GREEN}OpenLDAP is responsive.${NC}"
        break
    fi
    sleep 1
done

# Seed initial LDAP structure and AD groups if not already present
if ! docker exec "${LDAP_CONTAINER}" ldapsearch -x -H ldap://localhost -b "ou=users,dc=example,dc=org" -D "cn=admin,dc=example,dc=org" -w admin >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    echo "Configuring OpenLDAP memberof overlay for groupOfNames (bidirectional AD membership)..."
    docker exec -i "${LDAP_CONTAINER}" ldapmodify -Q -Y EXTERNAL -H ldapi:/// 2>/dev/null << 'EOF' || true
dn: olcOverlay={0}memberof,olcDatabase={1}mdb,cn=config
changetype: modify
replace: olcMemberOfGroupOC
olcMemberOfGroupOC: groupOfNames
-
replace: olcMemberOfMemberAD
olcMemberOfMemberAD: member
EOF

    echo "Seeding initial enterprise users and AD groups from k8s/ldap/bootstrap.ldif..."
    docker exec -i "${LDAP_CONTAINER}" ldapadd -x -D "cn=admin,dc=example,dc=org" -w admin < "${ROOT_DIR}/k8s/ldap/bootstrap.ldif"
    echo -e "${GREEN}OpenLDAP successfully seeded with enterprise users and AD security groups.${NC}"
else
    echo "OpenLDAP enterprise structure already initialized."
fi

echo -e "${CYAN}=== 2. Starting phpLDAPadmin Web GUI ===${NC}"
if ! docker ps --filter "name=^/${PHPLDAPADMIN_CONTAINER}$" --format '{{.Names}}' | grep -qx "${PHPLDAPADMIN_CONTAINER}"; then
    if docker ps -a --filter "name=^/${PHPLDAPADMIN_CONTAINER}$" --format '{{.Names}}' | grep -qx "${PHPLDAPADMIN_CONTAINER}"; then
        echo "Starting existing '${PHPLDAPADMIN_CONTAINER}' container..."
        docker start "${PHPLDAPADMIN_CONTAINER}"
    else
        echo "Launching external phpLDAPadmin container on port ${PHPLDAPADMIN_PORT}..."
        docker run -d \
            --name "${PHPLDAPADMIN_CONTAINER}" \
            --restart unless-stopped \
            -p "${PHPLDAPADMIN_PORT}:80" \
            -e PHPLDAPADMIN_LDAP_HOSTS="${LDAP_CONTAINER}" \
            -e PHPLDAPADMIN_HTTPS=false \
            osixia/phpldapadmin:0.9.0
    fi
else
    echo "External phpLDAPadmin container '${PHPLDAPADMIN_CONTAINER}' is already running."
fi

# Enable custom_attrs display (shows memberOf on users in templates)
docker exec -i "${PHPLDAPADMIN_CONTAINER}" bash -c '
    if ! grep -q "custom_attrs" /var/www/phpldapadmin/config/config.php; then
        echo "\$servers->setValue(\"server\",\"custom_attrs\",array(\"memberof\",\"memberOf\"));" >> /var/www/phpldapadmin/config/config.php
        service php7.3-fpm reload >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
    fi
' 2>/dev/null || true

if docker network inspect kind >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    docker network connect kind "${PHPLDAPADMIN_CONTAINER}" 2>/dev/null || true
fi

echo -e "${CYAN}=== 3. Mapping External OpenLDAP into Kubernetes ===${NC}"
if kubectl cluster-info >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    kubectl create namespace "${NAMESPACE}" --dry-run=client -o yaml | kubectl apply -f -
    kubectl apply -f "${ROOT_DIR}/k8s/ldap/service.yaml"
    echo -e "${GREEN}Service 'openldap' mapped in namespace '${NAMESPACE}' to external host container.${NC}"
else
    echo -e "${YELLOW}Kubernetes cluster not detected yet; skipping k8s service mapping.${NC}"
fi

echo -e "${GREEN}======================================================================${NC}"
echo -e "${GREEN}Enterprise OpenLDAP Directory is ready!${NC}"
echo -e "OpenLDAP Endpoint:  ldap://localhost:389 (Bind DN: cn=admin,dc=example,dc=org | Pass: admin)"
echo -e "phpLDAPadmin GUI:   http://localhost:8082 (Login DN: cn=admin,dc=example,dc=org | Pass: admin)"
echo -e "${GREEN}======================================================================${NC}"

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# ==============================================================================
# Setup Prometheus & Grafana Observability Stack for Airflow
# Scrapes Airflow StatsD metrics and displays cluster health in Grafana.
# ==============================================================================
set -euo pipefail

ROOT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
NAMESPACE="observability"

# Colors
CYAN='\033[0;36m'
GREEN='\033[0;32m'
YELLOW='\033[1;33m'
NC='\033[0m'

echo -e "${CYAN}=== Setting up Observability Stack (Prometheus + Grafana) ===${NC}"

# Apply Prometheus deployment, config, and network policy
echo "Deploying Prometheus..."
kubectl apply -f "${ROOT_DIR}/k8s/observability/prometheus.yaml"

# Sync Gmail SMTP secret to observability namespace for Grafana alerting
if kubectl get secret airflow-smtp-secret -n airflow >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    PASS=$(kubectl get secret airflow-smtp-secret -n airflow -o jsonpath='{.data.password}' | base64 -d)
    kubectl create secret generic grafana-smtp-secret \
        --from-literal=password="${PASS}" \
        -n "${NAMESPACE}" --dry-run=client -o yaml | kubectl apply -f -
fi

# Apply Grafana deployment, datasources, alerting rules, and dashboards
echo "Deploying Grafana..."
kubectl apply -f "${ROOT_DIR}/k8s/observability/grafana.yaml"

echo "Waiting for Prometheus and Grafana rollouts..."
kubectl rollout status deployment/prometheus -n "${NAMESPACE}" --timeout=120s
kubectl rollout status deployment/grafana -n "${NAMESPACE}" --timeout=120s

echo -e "${GREEN}Observability stack is healthy and running!${NC}"

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# ==============================================================================
# Setup & Seed External S3 Storage for Airflow
# Runs MinIO Object Storage as an external Docker container outside KinD cluster
# ==============================================================================
set -euo pipefail

ROOT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
MINIO_CONTAINER="airflow-minio"
MINIO_PORT="9000"
MINIO_CONSOLE_PORT="9001"
MINIO_USER="minioadmin"
MINIO_PASS="minioadmin"
NAMESPACE="airflow"

# Color helpers
CYAN='\033[0;36m'
GREEN='\033[0;32m'
YELLOW='\033[1;33m'
NC='\033[0m'

echo -e "${CYAN}=== 1. Checking External MinIO Container ===${NC}"
if ! docker ps --filter "name=^/${MINIO_CONTAINER}$" --format '{{.Names}}' | grep -qx "${MINIO_CONTAINER}"; then
    if docker ps -a --filter "name=^/${MINIO_CONTAINER}$" --format '{{.Names}}' | grep -qx "${MINIO_CONTAINER}"; then
        echo "Starting existing '${MINIO_CONTAINER}' container..."
        docker start "${MINIO_CONTAINER}"
    else
        echo "Launching new external MinIO container '${MINIO_CONTAINER}' on ports ${MINIO_PORT}/${MINIO_CONSOLE_PORT}..."
        docker run -d \
            --name "${MINIO_CONTAINER}" \
            --user root \
            --restart unless-stopped \
            -p "${MINIO_PORT}:9000" \
            -p "${MINIO_CONSOLE_PORT}:9001" \
            -v airflow-minio-data:/data \
            -e MINIO_ROOT_USER="${MINIO_USER}" \
            -e MINIO_ROOT_PASSWORD="${MINIO_PASS}" \
            cleanstart/minio:latest server /data --console-address ":9001"
    fi
else
    echo "MinIO container '${MINIO_CONTAINER}' is already running."
fi

# Connect MinIO to KinD network if present
if docker network inspect kind >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    docker network connect kind "${MINIO_CONTAINER}" 2>/dev/null || true
fi

echo "Waiting for MinIO to be ready at http://localhost:${MINIO_PORT}..."
for i in {1..30}; do
    if curl -s -f "http://localhost:${MINIO_PORT}/minio/health/live" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
        echo -e "${GREEN}MinIO storage is responsive.${NC}"
        break
    fi
    sleep 1
done

echo "Ensuring 'airflow-logs' and 'airflow-backups' buckets exist in MinIO..."
docker exec "${MINIO_CONTAINER}" mkdir -p /data/airflow-logs /data/airflow-backups

echo -e "${CYAN}=== 2. Connecting MinIO S3 to Kubernetes KinD Cluster ===${NC}"
if kubectl cluster-info >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    kubectl apply -f "${ROOT_DIR}/k8s/s3/service.yaml"
    echo -e "${GREEN}MinIO S3 service & endpoints mapped in namespace '${NAMESPACE}'.${NC}"
fi

echo -e "${GREEN}MinIO S3 API:     http://localhost:${MINIO_PORT}${NC}"
echo -e "${GREEN}MinIO Web Console: http://localhost:${MINIO_CONSOLE_PORT} (User: ${MINIO_USER} | Pass: ${MINIO_PASS})${NC}"

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# ==============================================================================
# Setup & Seed External HashiCorp Vault for Airflow
# Runs HashiCorp Vault as an external Docker container outside KinD cluster
# Configures Kubernetes Auth method for zero-secret ServiceAccount authentication
# ==============================================================================
set -euo pipefail

ROOT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
VAULT_CONTAINER="airflow-vault"
VAULT_PORT="8200"
VAULT_ADDR="http://localhost:${VAULT_PORT}"
VAULT_TOKEN="airflow-vault-root-token-2026"
NAMESPACE="airflow"

# Color helpers
CYAN='\033[0;36m'
GREEN='\033[0;32m'
YELLOW='\033[1;33m'
RED='\033[0;31m'
NC='\033[0m'

echo -e "${CYAN}=== 1. Checking External HashiCorp Vault Container ===${NC}"
if ! docker ps --filter "name=^/${VAULT_CONTAINER}$" --format '{{.Names}}' | grep -qx "${VAULT_CONTAINER}"; then
    if docker ps -a --filter "name=^/${VAULT_CONTAINER}$" --format '{{.Names}}' | grep -qx "${VAULT_CONTAINER}"; then
        echo "Starting existing '${VAULT_CONTAINER}' container..."
        docker start "${VAULT_CONTAINER}"
    else
        echo "Launching new external HashiCorp Vault container '${VAULT_CONTAINER}' on port ${VAULT_PORT}..."
        docker run -d \
            --name "${VAULT_CONTAINER}" \
            --restart unless-stopped \
            -p "${VAULT_PORT}:${VAULT_PORT}" \
            -e VAULT_DEV_ROOT_TOKEN_ID="${VAULT_TOKEN}" \
            -e VAULT_DEV_LISTEN_ADDRESS="0.0.0.0:${VAULT_PORT}" \
            -e VAULT_UI="true" \
            hashicorp/vault:1.15.6
    fi
else
    echo "HashiCorp Vault container '${VAULT_CONTAINER}' is already running."
fi

# Connect Vault container to KinD network if available so it can talk to the KinD API server
if docker network inspect kind >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    docker network connect kind "${VAULT_CONTAINER}" 2>/dev/null || true
fi

echo "Waiting for Vault to be ready at ${VAULT_ADDR}..."
for i in {1..30}; do
    if curl -s -f "${VAULT_ADDR}/v1/sys/health" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
        echo -e "${GREEN}Vault is healthy and responsive.${NC}"
        break
    fi
    sleep 1
done

echo -e "${CYAN}=== 2. Connecting Vault to Kubernetes KinD Cluster ===${NC}"
if kubectl cluster-info >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    kubectl create namespace "${NAMESPACE}" --dry-run=client -o yaml | kubectl apply -f -
    kubectl apply -f "${ROOT_DIR}/k8s/vault/service.yaml"
    echo -e "${GREEN}Vault service & endpoints mapped in namespace '${NAMESPACE}'.${NC}"

    echo -e "${CYAN}=== 2b. Configuring Vault Kubernetes Authentication Method ===${NC}"
    # Setup ServiceAccount and TokenReview permissions for Vault
    kubectl apply -f - <<EOF
apiVersion: v1
kind: ServiceAccount
metadata:
  name: vault-reviewer
  namespace: ${NAMESPACE}
---
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: ClusterRoleBinding
metadata:
  name: vault-reviewer-binding
roleRef:
  apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io
  kind: ClusterRole
  name: system:auth-delegator
subjects:
- kind: ServiceAccount
  name: vault-reviewer
  namespace: ${NAMESPACE}
---
apiVersion: v1
kind: Secret
metadata:
  name: vault-reviewer-token
  namespace: ${NAMESPACE}
  annotations:
    kubernetes.io/service-account.name: vault-reviewer
type: kubernetes.io/service-account-token
EOF

    sleep 1
    CA_CERT=$(kubectl get secret vault-reviewer-token -n "${NAMESPACE}" -o jsonpath='{.data.ca\.crt}' | base64 -d)
    REVIEWER_TOKEN=$(kubectl get secret vault-reviewer-token -n "${NAMESPACE}" -o jsonpath='{.data.token}' | base64 -d)

    # Enable and configure Kubernetes Auth in Vault
    docker exec -e VAULT_TOKEN="${VAULT_TOKEN}" -e VAULT_ADDR="http://127.0.0.1:${VAULT_PORT}" "${VAULT_CONTAINER}" \
        vault auth enable kubernetes 2>/dev/null || true

    docker exec -e VAULT_TOKEN="${VAULT_TOKEN}" -e VAULT_ADDR="http://127.0.0.1:${VAULT_PORT}" "${VAULT_CONTAINER}" \
        vault write auth/kubernetes/config \
        kubernetes_host="https://airflow-local-control-plane:6443" \
        kubernetes_ca_cert="${CA_CERT}" \
        token_reviewer_jwt="${REVIEWER_TOKEN}" >/dev/null

    # Create policy allowing Airflow to read secret/data/airflow/*
    docker exec -i -e VAULT_TOKEN="${VAULT_TOKEN}" -e VAULT_ADDR="http://127.0.0.1:${VAULT_PORT}" "${VAULT_CONTAINER}" \
        vault policy write airflow - <<'EOF'
path "secret/data/airflow/*" {
  capabilities = ["read"]
}
path "secret/metadata/airflow/*" {
  capabilities = ["read", "list"]
}
path "secret/data/airflow/variables/*" {
  capabilities = ["read"]
}
path "secret/data/airflow/connections/*" {
  capabilities = ["read"]
}
path "secret/data/airflow/config/*" {
  capabilities = ["read"]
}
EOF

    # Bind the policy to the Airflow ServiceAccounts
    docker exec -e VAULT_TOKEN="${VAULT_TOKEN}" -e VAULT_ADDR="http://127.0.0.1:${VAULT_PORT}" "${VAULT_CONTAINER}" \
        vault write auth/kubernetes/role/airflow \
        bound_service_account_names="airflow-*" \
        bound_service_account_namespaces="${NAMESPACE}" \
        policies="airflow" \
        ttl=24h >/dev/null

    echo -e "${GREEN}Vault Kubernetes auth configured (Role: airflow, bound to airflow-* ServiceAccounts).${NC}"
else
    echo -e "${YELLOW}Kubernetes cluster not detected yet; skipping k8s service mapping.${NC}"
fi

GMAIL_PASS="${GMAIL_APP_PASSWORD:-}"
if [[ -z "${GMAIL_PASS}" ]] && kubectl get secret airflow-smtp-secret -n "${NAMESPACE}" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    GMAIL_PASS=$(kubectl get secret airflow-smtp-secret -n "${NAMESPACE}" -o jsonpath='{.data.password}' 2>/dev/null | base64 -d || true)
fi

echo -e "${CYAN}=== 3. Populating Initial Secrets & Variables into Vault ===${NC}"
# Use Python helper to populate Vault KV v2 paths cleanly
GMAIL_APP_PASSWORD="${GMAIL_PASS}" python3 -c "
import urllib.request, json, os

vault_url = '${VAULT_ADDR}'
vault_token = '${VAULT_TOKEN}'
smtp_pwd = os.environ.get('GMAIL_APP_PASSWORD', '').replace(' ', '')

def write_secret(path, data):
    url = f'{vault_url}/v1/secret/data/{path}'
    payload = json.dumps({'data': data}).encode('utf-8')
    req = urllib.request.Request(
        url,
        data=payload,
        headers={'X-Vault-Token': vault_token, 'Content-Type': 'application/json'},
        method='POST'
    )
    try:
        urllib.request.urlopen(req)
        print(f'  [OK] secret/data/{path}')
    except Exception as e:
        print(f'  [ERROR] secret/data/{path}: {e}')

print('Seeding Airflow Variables:')
write_secret('airflow/variables/keycloak_admin_password', {'value': 'admin'})
write_secret('airflow/variables/keycloak_client_secret', {'value': 'airflow-client-secret'})
write_secret('airflow/variables/breakglass_password', {'value': 'ChangeMeInSecret123!'})
write_secret('airflow/variables/alert_recipient', {'value': 'alerts@example.com'})

print('Seeding Airflow Configs (Keys & Parameters):')
write_secret('airflow/config/core/fernet_key', {'value': '2SL0kSlBxSYcpJnwzhBCWFlGf0mxEao2='})
write_secret('airflow/config/webserver/secret_key', {'value': '2SL0kSlBxSYcpJnwzhBCWFlGf0mxEao2'})
write_secret('airflow/config/api/secret_key', {'value': '2SL0kSlBxSYcpJnwzhBCWFlGf0mxEao2'})

print('Seeding Airflow Connections:')
write_secret('airflow/connections/postgres_default', {
    'conn_type': 'postgres',
    'host': 'postgres.airflow.svc.cluster.local',
    'login': 'airflow',
    'password': 'airflow',
    'port': 5432,
    'schema': 'airflow'
})
write_secret('airflow/connections/smtp_default', {
    'conn_type': 'smtp',
    'host': 'smtp.gmail.com',
    'login': 'alerts@example.com',
    'password': smtp_pwd,
    'port': 587,
    'extra': json.dumps({
        'from_email': 'alerts@example.com',
        'disable_ssl': True,
        'disable_tls': False
    })
})
write_secret('airflow/connections/aws_default', {
    'conn_type': 'aws',
    'extra': json.dumps({
        'aws_access_key_id': 'minioadmin',
        'aws_secret_access_key': 'minioadmin',
        'endpoint_url': 'http://s3.airflow.svc.cluster.local:9000',
        'region_name': 'us-east-1'
    })

"

echo -e "${CYAN}=== 4. Syncing Airflow Secret Keys from Vault to Kubernetes ===${NC}"
if kubectl cluster-info >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    API_KEY=$(python3 -c "
import urllib.request, json
req = urllib.request.Request('${VAULT_ADDR}/v1/secret/data/airflow/config/api/secret_key', headers={'X-Vault-Token': '${VAULT_TOKEN}'})
print(json.loads(urllib.request.urlopen(req).read().decode())['data']['data']['value'])
")
    WEBSERVER_KEY=$(python3 -c "
import urllib.request, json
req = urllib.request.Request('${VAULT_ADDR}/v1/secret/data/airflow/config/webserver/secret_key', headers={'X-Vault-Token': '${VAULT_TOKEN}'})
print(json.loads(urllib.request.urlopen(req).read().decode())['data']['data']['value'])
")

    kubectl create secret generic airflow-api-secret \
        --from-literal=api-secret-key="${API_KEY}" \
        --from-literal=webserver-secret-key="${WEBSERVER_KEY}" \
        -n "${NAMESPACE}" --dry-run=client -o yaml | kubectl apply -f -
    echo -e "${GREEN}Kubernetes secret 'airflow-api-secret' synced from HashiCorp Vault.${NC}"
fi

echo ""
echo -e "${GREEN}======================================================================${NC}"
echo -e "${GREEN}HashiCorp Vault is running outside the cluster!${NC}"

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Quickstart script to run Apache Airflow locally on KinD (Kubernetes in Docker)
set -euo pipefail

CLUSTER_NAME="airflow-local"
NAMESPACE="airflow"
IMAGE_TAG="learn-airflow:3.3.2-python3.12"
ROOT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"

echo "=== 1. Checking KinD Cluster ==="
if ! kind get clusters 2>/dev/null | grep -qx "${CLUSTER_NAME}"; then
    echo "Creating KinD cluster '${CLUSTER_NAME}' with port mappings (8080 -> 30080)..."
    kind create cluster --config "${ROOT_DIR}/k8s/kind-cluster.yaml"
else
    echo "KinD cluster '${CLUSTER_NAME}' is already running."
fi

echo "=== 2. Building & Loading Docker Image into KinD ==="
echo "Building ${IMAGE_TAG} with authlib..."
docker build -t "${IMAGE_TAG}" "${ROOT_DIR}"
echo "Loading ${IMAGE_TAG} into ${CLUSTER_NAME}..."
kind load docker-image "${IMAGE_TAG}" --name "${CLUSTER_NAME}"

echo "=== 3. Setting Up Namespace & ConfigMaps ==="
kubectl create namespace "${NAMESPACE}" --dry-run=client -o yaml | kubectl apply -f -
kubectl create configmap airflow-webserver-config \
    --from-file=webserver_config.py="${ROOT_DIR}/config/webserver_config.py" \
    -n "${NAMESPACE}" --dry-run=client -o yaml | kubectl apply -f -

echo "=== 3a. Starting & Mapping External OpenLDAP Enterprise Directory ==="
"${ROOT_DIR}/scripts/setup-ldap.sh"

echo "=== 3b. Starting & Mapping External Keycloak Identity Provider ==="
"${ROOT_DIR}/scripts/setup-keycloak.sh"

echo "=== 3c. Starting & Mapping External PostgreSQL & Redis ==="
"${ROOT_DIR}/scripts/setup-db-redis.sh"

echo "=== 3d. Starting & Mapping External HashiCorp Vault ==="
"${ROOT_DIR}/scripts/setup-vault.sh"

echo "=== 3e. Starting & Mapping External S3 Storage ==="
"${ROOT_DIR}/scripts/setup-s3.sh"


echo "=== 4. Checking Gmail SMTP Secret ==="
if ! kubectl get secret airflow-smtp-secret -n "${NAMESPACE}" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    echo "----------------------------------------------------------------------"
    echo "NOTICE: Secret 'airflow-smtp-secret' not found in namespace '${NAMESPACE}'."
    echo "To send failure emails from alerts@example.com, a 16-char Google App Password is required."
    echo ""
    GMAIL_PASS="${GMAIL_APP_PASSWORD:-}"
    if [[ -z "${GMAIL_PASS}" ]]; then
        if [[ -t 0 ]]; then
            echo "If you have your App Password now, enter it below (or press Enter to create a placeholder):"
            read -r -s -p "Gmail 16-char App Password: " GMAIL_PASS
            echo ""
        fi
    fi
    if [[ -z "${GMAIL_PASS}" ]]; then
        GMAIL_PASS="REPLACE_WITH_YOUR_16_CHAR_GMAIL_APP_PASSWORD"
        echo "Created placeholder secret. Update it with your real password using:"
        echo "  kubectl create secret generic airflow-smtp-secret --from-literal=password=\"<APP_PWD>\" -n ${NAMESPACE} --dry-run=client -o yaml | kubectl apply -f -"
    fi
    kubectl create secret generic airflow-smtp-secret \
        --from-literal=password="${GMAIL_PASS}" \
        -n "${NAMESPACE}"
else
    echo "Secret 'airflow-smtp-secret' already exists."
fi

echo "=== 4b. Checking Git-Sync SSH Secret ==="
if ! kubectl get secret airflow-ssh-secret -n "${NAMESPACE}" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    SSH_KEY_FILE="${HOME}/.ssh/id_ed25519"
    if [[ ! -f "${SSH_KEY_FILE}" ]]; then
        SSH_KEY_FILE="${HOME}/.ssh/id_rsa"
    fi
    if [[ -f "${SSH_KEY_FILE}" ]]; then
        kubectl create secret generic airflow-ssh-secret \
            --from-file=gitSshKey="${SSH_KEY_FILE}" \
            -n "${NAMESPACE}"
        echo "Created 'airflow-ssh-secret' from ${SSH_KEY_FILE}."
    else
        echo "WARNING: No SSH key found at ~/.ssh/id_ed25519 or ~/.ssh/id_rsa for git-sync."
    fi
else
    echo "Secret 'airflow-ssh-secret' already exists."
fi

echo "=== 4c. Checking Break-Glass Admin Password Secret ==="
BREAKGLASS_SECRET="airflow-breakglass-secret"
BREAKGLASS_PASS="${AIRFLOW_ADMIN_PASSWORD:-ChangeMeInSecret123!}"
if ! kubectl get secret "${BREAKGLASS_SECRET}" -n "${NAMESPACE}" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    kubectl create secret generic "${BREAKGLASS_SECRET}" \
        --from-literal=password="${BREAKGLASS_PASS}" \
        -n "${NAMESPACE}"
    echo "Created '${BREAKGLASS_SECRET}' in namespace '${NAMESPACE}'."
else
    BREAKGLASS_PASS=$(kubectl get secret "${BREAKGLASS_SECRET}" -n "${NAMESPACE}" -o jsonpath='{.data.password}' | base64 -d)
fi

echo "=== 5. Deploying Airflow with Helm ==="
helm repo add apache-airflow https://airflow.apache.org 2>/dev/null || true
helm repo update apache-airflow

helm upgrade --install airflow apache-airflow/airflow \
    -f "${ROOT_DIR}/values.yaml" \
    --set-string createUserJob.defaultUser.password="${BREAKGLASS_PASS}" \
    --namespace "${NAMESPACE}"

echo "Waiting for scheduler and api-server pods..."
kubectl rollout status deployment/airflow-scheduler -n "${NAMESPACE}" --timeout=300s
kubectl rollout status deployment/airflow-api-server -n "${NAMESPACE}" --timeout=300s

echo "Ensuring AD team roles exist in Airflow database..."
kubectl exec -n "${NAMESPACE}" deploy/airflow-scheduler -c scheduler -- airflow roles create aid-finance-auth 2>/dev/null || true
kubectl exec -n "${NAMESPACE}" deploy/airflow-scheduler -c scheduler -- airflow roles create aid-marketing-auth 2>/dev/null || true
kubectl exec -n "${NAMESPACE}" deploy/airflow-scheduler -c scheduler -- airflow roles create aid-logistics-auth 2>/dev/null || true
kubectl exec -n "${NAMESPACE}" deploy/airflow-scheduler -c scheduler -- airflow roles create aid-analytics-auth 2>/dev/null || true

echo "Seeding base operational permissions for AD team roles..."
kubectl exec -n "${NAMESPACE}" deploy/airflow-scheduler -c scheduler -- python3 -c '
from airflow.providers.fab.auth_manager.models import Role, Permission, Action, Resource
from airflow.settings import Session

session = Session()
roles = ["aid-finance-auth", "aid-marketing-auth", "aid-logistics-auth", "aid-analytics-auth"]
base_perms = [
    ("can_read", "Asset Aliases"), ("can_read", "Assets"), ("menu_access", "Assets"),
    ("can_read", "Backfills"), ("menu_access", "Browse"), ("can_read", "Cluster Activity"),
    ("menu_access", "Cluster Activity"), ("can_read", "DAG Code"), ("can_read", "DAG Dependencies"),
    ("menu_access", "DAG Dependencies"), ("can_read", "DAG Runs"), ("can_create", "DAG Runs"),
    ("can_edit", "DAG Runs"), ("can_delete", "DAG Runs"), ("menu_access", "DAG Runs"),
    ("can_read", "DAG Versions"), ("can_read", "DAG Warnings"), ("menu_access", "DAGs"),
    ("menu_access", "Docs"), ("menu_access", "Documentation"), ("can_read", "HITL Detail"),
    ("can_edit", "HITL Detail"), ("can_read", "ImportError"),
    ("can_read", "Jobs"), ("menu_access", "Jobs"), ("can_edit", "My Password"),
    ("can_read", "My Password"), ("can_edit", "My Profile"), ("can_read", "My Profile"),
    ("can_read", "Pools"), ("can_read", "Plugins"), ("can_read", "SLA Misses"),
    ("menu_access", "SLA Misses"), ("can_read", "Task Instances"), ("can_create", "Task Instances"),
    ("can_edit", "Task Instances"), ("can_delete", "Task Instances"), ("menu_access", "Task Instances"),
    ("can_read", "Task Logs"), ("can_read", "Website"), ("can_read", "XComs"),
]
for r_name in roles:
    r = session.query(Role).filter(Role.name == r_name).first()
    if r:
        for a_name, res_name in base_perms:
            p = session.query(Permission).join(Action).join(Resource).filter(Action.name == a_name, Resource.name == res_name).first()
            if p and p not in r.permissions:
                r.permissions.append(p)
session.commit()
' 2>/dev/null || true

echo "Seeding department execution pools with isolated worker slot quotas..."
for dept in finance marketing logistics analytics; do
    kubectl exec -n "${NAMESPACE}" deploy/airflow-scheduler -c scheduler -- \
        airflow pools set "${dept}_pool" 8 "${dept^} department execution pool" 2>/dev/null || true
done


echo "=== 6. Deploying Ingress & TLS (cert-manager) ==="

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Tear down the local KinD Kubernetes cluster
set -euo pipefail

CLUSTER_NAME="airflow-local"

echo "=== Deleting KinD cluster '${CLUSTER_NAME}' ==="
if kind get clusters 2>/dev/null | grep -qx "${CLUSTER_NAME}"; then
    kind delete cluster --name "${CLUSTER_NAME}"
    echo "KinD cluster '${CLUSTER_NAME}' deleted successfully."
else
    echo "KinD cluster '${CLUSTER_NAME}' is not currently running."
fi

echo "=== Stopping external infrastructure containers ==="
docker stop airflow-keycloak airflow-openldap airflow-phpldapadmin airflow-postgres airflow-redis airflow-vault airflow-minio 2>/dev/null || true
echo "All external containers stopped. Volumes and metadata are preserved."

"""Test package."""

[55/56] FILE: tests/test_dag_governance.py
SIZE: 9.9 KB (10125 bytes)
